Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The extraction operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 11. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 539 with 1 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the convergence analysis condition on extraction structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 11. The convergence follows from the convergence analysis estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 539. QED.

Theorem 212.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the extraction space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 87 and the convergence rate is O(94/n^4).

Proof: The structural decomposition estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 87 + O(1/n^94). Summing over all modes and applying the extraction structural decomposition criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 212.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any extraction module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 61. The projective resolution has length at most 871 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the optimality condition decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_61(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 61. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^871(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 212.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: For any extraction module M satisfying the invariance principle hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 71. The projective resolution has length at most 140 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: We apply the invariance principle principle to the extraction decomposition. By minimality, the element a^71 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 140-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 212.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: For any extraction module M satisfying the spectral property hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 830. The projective resolution has length at most 949 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: By the spectral property principle applied to extraction theory, we decompose the problem into 830 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the spectral property fixed-point theorem with constant 949. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.
